[2026-09-01T00:22:29.164Z] [world] "Everyone, twice a season; nobody, the rest of it." Halem counts on his fingers. "Felra for boots — go to her, do not let me sell you boots. Irma if you plan to be hit. Tellin if you want a fair number said to your face." He grins. "And me for everything in between, which is where all the living actually happens."
[2026-09-01T00:22:29.862Z] [player] Ask about his father
[2026-09-01T00:22:29.864Z] [world] "He kept the front room open through the worst three years anyone remembers." Halem is quieter than usual. "Not because he was brave. Because he could not think of anything else to do, and it turned out a lit window was worth more than everything on the shelves." He wipes the counter. "People still call this place by his name sometimes. I have never once corrected them."
[2026-09-01T00:22:30.511Z] [player] Ask who keeps his books
[2026-09-01T00:22:30.512Z] [world] "Sera. My wife." Halem says it with the particular pride of a man who knows he married up. "You will never see her at the counter — she says the counter is theatre and she does not perform. But every price I quote, she set, and every time I have overruled her it has cost me." He leans in. "She priced YOU, by the way. First week. I am not telling you the number, but she has not changed it, which for Sera is a love letter."
[2026-09-01T00:22:31.144Z] [player] Ask why he never left the crossroads
[2026-09-01T00:22:31.146Z] [world] "I did leave. Once. Half a year, my seventeenth — I went with a wagon crew to see the coast everyone talked about." Halem straightens tins that are straight. "The coast is a line where the mud meets more mud, wetter. And the whole way back I kept thinking: every road in Tartaria crosses every other road SOMEWHERE, and my father was already standing on it." He shrugs. "You do not leave the middle of the map. You just stop noticing you are standing on it."

[2026-09-01T00:22:32.578Z] [player] Ask again about the arithmetic he does not like
[2026-09-01T00:22:32.580Z] [world] The cheer goes all the way down this time. "Goods moving without people means somebody is moving them who does not stop at posts. Does not trade. Does not need to." Halem keeps his voice pleasant for the room, but his eyes are not pleasant. "I have kept a light in this window my whole life on the understanding that the road wants to stop. If something is out there that does not want to stop —" He taps the counter twice. "Then I am not a trading post. I am a lit window on its route."
[2026-09-01T00:22:33.328Z] [player] Ask about the cart that never arrived
[2026-09-01T00:22:33.329Z] [world] Halem checks the room before he answers, which from him is remarkable. "Two seasons back I paid for a cart of good stock out of the north fork. It never arrived. The driver did — three days late, boots ruined, no cart, and no story. Not lying. MISSING the story, like a page cut out." He slides a marked scrap of map across the counter. "He drew me where he thinks he was. He will not go back and I cannot leave. The stock is long gone, I know that. What I will pay for is knowing what is on that stretch of road."
